Let me build the plan for the **Real-Time Collaborative Document Editor**.\n\n---\n\n# BUILD PLAN: Real-Time Collaborative Document Editor\n\n## 1. Classification and constraints\n\n**What is being built**: A real-time collaborative document editor (synchronous multi-user editing, e.g., Google Docs / Notion style).\n\n**Type**: Software (distributed system: frontend + backend + database).\n\n**Greenfield or brownfield**: Greenfield (new system).\n\n**Scale**: Assumed not-small. Collaborative editors face high concurrency: typical assumption is 100–1000 concurrent users, documents with 10k–1M characters, 10–100 edits per second under peak. Conflict resolution and network partition handling are mandatory.\n\n**Dominant constraint**: **Correctness > Availability > Latency > Cost**. Concurrent edits must merge without data loss; network partitions and crashes must not corrupt the document. Latency matters (users expect <200ms end-to-end), but data integrity is non-negotiable.\n\n**Worst failure**: Data corruption or loss during concurrent edits; or unauthorized access to private documents.\n\n**Budgets that matter** (labeled):\n- **Latency**: p99 keystroke-to-render <200ms (ASSUMPTION: derived from user expectations, not stated in request; confirm with product owner).\n- **Throughput**: 50+ concurrent users per document (ASSUMPTION: typical collaborative editing; spike test required).\n- **Availability**: 99.5% uptime (ASSUMPTION: not a safety-critical system, so 99.5% is defensible; SaaS standard is 99.9%, but cost differs).\n- **Storage**: Full edit history, no limit (ASSUMPTION: unlimited history; cost model TBD in Phase 3).\n- **Infrastructure cost**: <$0.10/concurrent-user/month (ASSUMPTION: competitive with existing players; validate in V10).\n- **RTO (Recovery Time Objective)**: 1 hour (ASSUMPTION: acceptable for non-critical tool).\n- **RPO (Recovery Point Objective)**: 15 minutes (ASSUMPTION: lose ≤15min of edits on total failure).\n\n**Missing inputs that would change the plan**:\n1. **Real scale requirements** (how many users, documents, concurrent edits?): Supplied by product owner before Phase 1. If HIPAA, Phase 2 adds encryption design; Phase 5 adds audit logging.\n3. **Team skill and size**: Assumed 4–6 engineers (full-stack capable). If smaller, Phase 0–1 may merge; if larger, parallel tracks possible.\n4. **Timeline**: Not stated. Assumed 6–9 months to Phase 8 (public launch); if compressed to 3 months, methodolgy exceptions E1 (spike before skeleton) and E2 (parallel tracks) apply.\n\n**Assumption: no existing system to integrate**. If there is a legacy document store or auth system, this becomes brownfield (Phase 0 runs behind a flag, Phase 5 does cutover). Plan assumes greenfield unless told otherwise.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Dependency map\n\n**Structural**:\n- **Conflict-resolution algorithm** blocks all downstream editor code. Choice of CRDT vs OT is irreversible once data is in the database (R3).\n- **API contract** (WebSocket message schema, REST endpoints) blocks client and server implementation.\n- **Document data model** (schema for users, docs, operations, versions) blocks persistence and recovery.\n- **Authentication scheme** (JWT, OAuth, sessions) blocks authorization and audit logging.\n\n**Runtime**:\n- **Database** must be running before server can persist edits.\n- **Real-time transport** (WebSocket) must be deployed before clients can sync live.\n- **Identity provider** (if external, e.g., Okta) must be up before production login.\n\n**Decision** (hard-to-reverse tradeoffs):\n- **CRDT vs Operational Transform (OT)**: Affects merge semantics, offline support, latency. R3.\n- **Self-hosted vs managed PaaS** (Firebase, Firestore, Replicache): Affects data sovereignty, cost predictability, operational burden. R3.\n- **Document versioning**: Full history vs snapshots + deltas. R3 (storage model, query patterns).\n- **Consistency model**: Strong (Raft consensus) vs eventual (CRDT + async). R3 (user experience, conflict handling).\n- **Monolith vs microservices**: R3 if public API will be exposed; but can defer to Phase 7 if not.\n\n**Validation**:\n- **CRDT merge correctness** must be proven before real documents exist (V1).\n- **Latency under load** must hit SLO before scaling to many users (V6).\n- **No data loss under network partition** must be tested (V9 on chaos).\n- **Authorization gates** must be audited before multi-user sharing (V8).\n\n**Risk / Security**:\n- **Authentication** must guard document access (risk: unauthorized read/write) — must pass V5 before Phase 3 real documents.\n- **Authorization checks** (per-document permissions) must guard exposure — must pass V8 before Phase 5 sharing.\n- **Transport encryption (TLS)** must be in place before real user data. Baseline in Phase 0 (internal), hardened in Phase 7.\n- **Data isolation** (one user cannot see another's docs) must be enforced before public launch (V11).\n\n**Organizational**:\n- **Product owner decision** on scale / compliance requirements: needed before Phase 1 (lead time 1–2 weeks if not ready).\n- **Security review** (internal or external audit): needed before Phase 8 public launch; lead time 4–6 weeks.\n- **DevOps / SRE** readiness (infrastructure, observability, on-call): needed for Phase 7 production scale.\n\n**Economic**:\n- **Real-time infrastructure cost model** (V2 operational spike): needed before committing to self-hosted (vs PaaS flip).\n- **Storage cost** (full history vs snapshots): needed before scaling beyond 100k docs (V3 storage spike).\n\n**No instances**:\n- No brownfield system (greenfield assumed; revised if legacy integration discovered).\n- No AI / agentic layer (no LLM, no guardrails, no orchestration).\n\n---\n\n## 3. Tradeoff gates\n\n| Decision | Reversibility | Default | Assumption | Validated by | Flip condition |\n|----------|---|---------|-----------|--------------|---|\n| **CRDT vs Operational Transform** | R3 (data migration, user experience break) | **CRDT** (Yjs library) — simpler merge semantics, offline-first, industry trend | CRDT performance is acceptable (<500ms latency under conflict storms); team can operate Yjs correctly | **V1**: Spike 2-user Yjs editor, 50 concurrent edits, p99 latency <500ms, zero data loss | V1 fails (p99 >1s or complexity >2 weeks) → switch to proven OT library (ShareDB) |\n| **Self-hosted vs managed PaaS** | R3 (vendor lock-in, data migration, cost structure) | **Self-hosted** (Node.js + WebSocket + PostgreSQL) — control, compliance flexibility, cost predictable at scale | Team can operate real-time infrastructure; compliance does not mandate external provider; cost per user <$0.10 at 1k users | **V2**: 1-week staging canary (50-user synthetic load), measure uptime ≥99%, cost model identified, on-call response <15min | V2 fails (uptime <99% or cost model >$0.15/user) → flip to Firebase Realtime DB or Replicache + managed backend |\n| **Full history vs snapshots + deltas** | R3 (affects audit trail, storage model, undo/redo) | **Full CRDT history** (every edit operation stored immutably) — enables infinite undo, audit trail, compliance | Storage cost acceptable (<$1/doc/month); DB query latency acceptable (<2s for \"show history\") | **V3**: Simulate 3-month doc lifecycle (insert/delete/format ops), measure DB size, query p99 latency for \"get history\", cost/doc | V3 fails (cost >$1/doc or query p99 >2s) → flip to periodic snapshots + delta compression |\n| **Consistency model** | R3 (affects conflict UX, network partition handling) | **Eventual consistency** (CRDT merge, no blocking) — offline support, always available, auto-resolve conflicts | Users tolerate 100–500ms lag between clients; automatic merges produce sensible results (no invisible data loss) | **V4**: Conflict scenario test (100 realistic patterns: concurrent insert/delete, format conflicts), reconstruct doc, zero data loss, merge quality acceptable | V4 fails (data loss or merge quality poor) → add Raft consensus layer for strong consistency (slower, blocks offline) |\n| **WebSocket vs polling** | R2 (deployment, performance) | **WebSocket** (bidirectional, low latency, persistent connection) — industry standard for real-time | Infrastructure supports persistent connections; firewalls permit WebSocket upgrade (fallback to polling if not) | Cheap check: load test 10k concurrent WS connections, measure memory/CPU, latency p99 <200ms | Load test fails (memory >500MB/1k conn or latency >500ms) → add polling fallback, prioritize WS |\n\n**R1 decisions** (one line):\n- Use TypeScript for type safety and shared schemas between client/server. Use Docker + Kubernetes for reproducibility and scaling.\n\n**N/A**:\n- **Build vs buy for CRDT**: Not a tradeoff. Yjs is proven, open-source, industry standard; building from scratch is not viable.\n- **Monolith vs microservices**: Deferred to Phase 7 (if public API needed); greenfield monolith is simpler (decouple later if needed).\n\n---\n\n## 4. Walking skeleton (Phase 0)\n\n**Who can reach it**: Internal developers and QA only. Behind feature flag `editor_v1`. No real user data. No production database access until V0 passes.\n\n**One real request end-to-end**:\n1. **Client**: Open browser, navigate to `http://localhost:3000/editor?doc_id=test123`\n2. **Client**: JavaScript initiates WebSocket upgrade to `ws://localhost:3001/sync`\n3. **Server**: Receives upgrade, creates session, verifies internal (no auth yet, but request is from internal IP)\n4. **Server**: Fetches doc state from database (initial CRDT state = empty)\n5. **Server**: Sends initial state to client: `{ type: \"init\", state: {...} }`\n6. **Client**: Receives state, initializes local CRDT, renders empty editor\n7. **User**: Types \"hello world\" (6 keystrokes)\n8. **Client**: For each keystroke, generates CRDT insert operation, sends to server: `{ type: \"update\", op: { pos: X, content: \"c\" } }`\n9. **Server**: Receives update, applies to document CRDT, broadcasts to all connected clients (including sender), persists to `document_updates` table\n10. **All clients**: Receive broadcast, apply op to local CRDT, re-render (should show \"hello world\" live)\n11. **Server**: Logs trace: `session:open → update:received → crdt:apply → db:write → broadcast:sent`\n12. **Latency**: <100ms local (keystroke to own client render), <200ms remote (keystroke to other client render)\n\n**Deployment**:\n- Docker Compose (`docker-compose.yml`):\n  - `server` (Node.js + Express + `ws` library + Yjs)\n  - `db` (PostgreSQL 14)\n  - `adminer` (for local DB inspection)\n- GitHub Actions pipeline:\n  1. `npm ci` (install exact versions)\n  2. `npm test` (unit tests)\n  3. `docker build` (server image)\n  4. `docker push` (to ECR, or local registry for local CI)\n  5. Deploy to `localhost:3000` (for local dev) or ECS/K8s cluster (for staging)\n\n**Logging (structured, JSON)**:\n- Session lifecycle: `{ ts, event: \"session:open\", session_id, user_id:\"internal\", doc_id }`\n- Update received: `{ ts, event: \"update:received\", op_count, op_size_bytes }`\n- CRDT apply: `{ ts, event: \"crdt:apply\", op_id, latency_ms, merge_conflict: false }`\n- DB write: `{ ts, event: \"db:write\", latency_ms, table: \"document_updates\" }`\n- Broadcast: `{ ts, event: \"broadcast:sent\", client_count, latency_ms }`\n- Errors: `{ ts, level: \"error\", component, message, stack_trace }`\n\n**Monitoring (dashboards in CloudWatch / Datadog)**:\n- **Latency histogram**: keystroke → server receive (network) + server apply (CRDT) + server broadcast (network) + client render. P50, P95, P99.\n- **WebSocket status**: active connections, disconnected, reconnect rate.\n- **Database metrics**: write latency (p99), connection pool utilization.\n- **CRDT metrics**: conflict rate (per-minute), merge operation count, state size growth over time.\n- **Memory / CPU**: per-connection baseline (should be <1MB/conn for skeleton).\n- **Errors**: exception rate, error type breakdown.\n\n**Rollback**:\n1. Feature flag `editor_v1` set to false → all requests route to old (or no-op) editor.\n2. Database schema rolled back (migration reversible; schema only adds `document_updates` table, does not alter existing).\n3. Docker image reverted: `docker pull old_image_sha` → re-deploy.\n4. Verification: old clients continue to work; no data loss.\n\n**Exit check V0** (all must pass):\n1. ✅ **Real request succeeds**: User creates doc, types 50 characters in one client, second client sees live update within 200ms, first client sees reflection of their own edit.\n2. ✅ **Deploy repeatable**: Run full pipeline 3 times in a row; each deploy succeeds, starts, and old clients still work.\n3. ✅ **Rollback succeeds**: Rollback to previous image, verify system still responds; re-deploy, system comes back.\n4. ✅ **Injected failure alerts**: Kill server process; alert fires in CloudWatch within 30 seconds.\n5. ✅ **Baselines recorded**: Latency histogram (p50, p99) at 1 user / 1 doc; memory baseline (~50MB).\n\n---\n\n## 5. Phases\n\n### **Phase 1: Conflict resolution and data model validation**\n\n**What it unlocks**: Confirmed merge strategy; finalized document schema; unblocked build of core logic.\n\n**Depends on**: V0 passes (skeleton is stable); product owner confirms scale targets.\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **V1 spike: CRDT correctness under load**\n   - Implement toy 2-user Yjs editor (React + Yjs, no server)\n   - Generate 50 random concurrent edits (inserts, deletes, concurrent at same position)\n   - Measure: latency p99, operation count before/after merge, data loss incidents\n   - Success: p99 <500ms, zero data loss\n2. **V2 spike: Operational readiness**\n   - Deploy Phase 0 skeleton to staging with 1-week continuous 50-user synthetic load (bots editing same doc continuously)\n   - Measure: uptime (target 99%+), infrastructure cost ($/user/month), on-call response time (<15min)\n   - Document: cost model, scaling bottlenecks (CPU, memory, DB), connection lifecycle\n3. **V3 spike: Storage and query cost**\n   - Simulate 3-month document lifecycle: 10k edits per doc, varied sizes (1KB–1MB final size)\n   - Measure: DB table size, query latency for \"get all edits for doc\", cost per doc at various scales\n   - Success: cost <$1/doc/month, query p99 <2s\n4. **V4 spike: Conflict merge quality**\n   - Generate 100 realistic conflict scenarios (concurrent insert/delete/format at overlapping positions)\n   - Reconstruct doc from CRDT operations, verify: zero data loss, no unintended duplicates, merge is deterministic\n   - Success: zero data loss, merge result matches expected\n5. **Document data model (schema design)**\n   - `users` table: `id, email, created_at`\n   - `documents` table: `id, owner_id, title, created_at, updated_at`\n   - `document_updates` table: `id, doc_id, op_id (ULID), client_id, op (JSON CRDT op), created_at, applied_at` — immutable append-only log\n   - `sessions` table: `id, user_id, token_hash, created_at, expires_at`\n   - Indexes: `(doc_id, op_id)` for fast replay, `(user_id, created_at)` for user's docs\n6. **API contract sketch**\n   - `POST /documents` → create doc, return `{ id, state }`\n   - `GET /documents/:id` → fetch doc state (reconstructed from ops)\n   - `WebSocket /sync` → persistent connection for real-time updates; messages: `{ type: \"update\", op }, { type: \"init\", state }`\n\n**Rollback**: All spikes are throw-away code. If V1–V4 fail, go back to step 3 (Tradeoff gates) and re-plan with different defaults (e.g., OT instead of CRDT, Firebase instead of self-hosted).\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V1, V2, V3, V4 all pass (or flip condition triggered and re-plan complete)\n- ✅ Data model reviewed and accepted\n- ✅ API sketch documented\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 2: Authentication and authorization blueprint**\n\n**What it unlocks**: Spec for auth system; permission model; ready to build hardened implementation.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 1 complete; product owner confirms compliance requirements (GDPR, SOC2, etc.).\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Design auth system**\n   - **Token strategy**: JWT (stateless, scalable) vs sessions (stateful, simpler)\n   - **Default**: JWT with 15min access token + 7-day refresh token (refresh allows long sessions without leaking access token)\n   - **Refresh token rotation**: New refresh token issued with each refresh; old token invalidated\n   - **Implementation**: Node.js `jsonwebtoken` library, RS256 (asymmetric signing for scalability), keys in environment\n2. **Design permission model**\n   - **Roles**: `owner`, `editor`, `viewer`, `commenter` (commenter deferred to Phase 5)\n   - **Owner**: create doc, delete doc, change permissions, edit content\n   - **Editor**: edit content, view edit history\n   - **Viewer**: read-only, cannot edit\n   - **Storage**: `document_permissions` table: `doc_id, user_id, role, granted_by, granted_at`\n3. **Design WebSocket auth**\n   - Client sends JWT in WebSocket upgrade header: `Upgrade: websocket, Authorization: Bearer <token>`\n   - Server verifies token on upgrade; rejects if invalid or expired\n   - Periodic token refresh: client sends refresh token, server issues new access token (without reconnect needed)\n4. **V5 gate: Authentication cannot be bypassed**\n   - Test: attempt WebSocket connect without token → rejected 401\n   - Test: attempt with expired token → rejected 401\n   - Test: attempt to call `POST /update` (if added) without token → rejected 401\n   - Test: with valid token, call succeeds\n   - Success: 100% of unauthenticated requests rejected, zero data leakage\n5. **Password policy and reset**\n   - Passwords hashed with bcrypt (cost factor 12)\n   - Password reset via email link (expires in 1h, single-use)\n   - No plain-text passwords in logs\n\n**Rollback**: Auth logic is in middleware; disable entire auth layer during Phase 2 by setting `SKIP_AUTH=true` in config (internal use only).\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V5 passes (auth gate)\n- ✅ Auth design document reviewed and approved\n- ✅ Permission matrix tested in unit tests\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 3: Core editor backend (CRDT sync, database persistence)**\n\n**What it unlocks**: Real documents, production data, live collaboration.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 2 complete, V5 passes (auth is in place).\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Implement CRDT state machine**\n   - Integrate Yjs library: `const ydoc = new Y.Doc(), ytext = ydoc.getText(\"content\")`\n   - Track operation sequence: each insert/delete/format generates CRDT operation\n   - Implement state reconstruction: given all historical ops, replay and reconstruct doc state\n   - Test: 5 concurrent clients, 200 edits each (1000 total), verify all clients converge to same state\n2. **Implement WebSocket server**\n   - Use `ws` library (Node.js)\n   - On client connect: authenticate token, load doc state from DB, send initial state\n   - On message receive: apply to CRDT, broadcast to all clients connected to this doc, persist to DB\n   - Heartbeat every 30s (keep-alive)\n   - On disconnect: clean up session, mark client as offline (but keep doc state)\n3. **Implement database persistence**\n   - Persist every update: `INSERT INTO document_updates (doc_id, op_id, op, created_at) VALUES (...)`\n   - Bulk inserts (batch every 10 ops or 100ms) for efficiency\n   - Transaction: apply op to CRDT + insert to DB atomically (serializable isolation)\n4. **Implement document fetch (reconstruct from history)**\n   - `GET /documents/:id/state`: SELECT all ops from `document_updates` WHERE doc_id = :id, replay in order, return final state\n   - Cache final state as snapshot (if V3 spike confirmed snapshots needed) to avoid full replay on every fetch\n5. **Implement rate-limiting**\n   - Per-user: max 10 edits/second (prevent abuse)\n   - Per-document: max 100 edits/second (sanity check)\n6. **V6 gate: Correctness and latency under load**\n   - Test: 5 concurrent clients, each sends 200 random edits, measure end-to-end latency (keystroke to all clients render), run for 10 min\n   - Check: zero data loss (reconstruct all clients' state, verify identical), zero crashes, p99 latency <200ms\n   - Success: p50 <100ms, p99 <200ms, zero data loss\n\n**Rollback**: Database migration is reversible (new table only); revert code, migration automatically rolls back on redeploy.\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V6 passes (performance gate)\n- ✅ Stress test passed (chaos test: kill server mid-sync, restart, verify recovery)\n- ✅ Latency baselines recorded\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 4: Client-side editor UI (end-to-end real-time editing)**\n\n**What it unlocks**: User-facing editor experience, internal user acceptance testing.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 3 complete, V6 passes (backend is stable and fast).\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Implement React editor component**\n   - Use CodeMirror or ProseMirror (proven editors with real-time support)\n   - Integrate Yjs: `const binding = new CodemirrorBinding(ytext, cm.view, new Set([cm, provider]))`\n   - Two-way sync: local edits → Yjs → WebSocket → server → other clients\n2. **Implement WebSocket client**\n   - Auto-reconnect with exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s, ..., max 30s)\n   - Offline queue: if connection drops, queue edits locally, replay on reconnect\n   - Conflict resolution: CRDT handles automatically, no user intervention needed\n3. **Implement cursor presence (show other users' cursors)**\n   - Broadcast cursor position on keystroke / mouse click (throttled, max 1 per 100ms)\n   - Subscribe to other users' cursors, draw in UI (e.g., color-coded carets)\n   - V7 gate: cursor latency p99 <300ms, smooth visual experience\n4. **Implement local undo/redo**\n   - Yjs undo manager: `const uiManager = new UndoManager([ytext])`\n   - Undo respects CRDT semantics: undo an insert, even if other user deleted later chars\n5. **Implement offline support**\n   - Client queues edits while offline (in IndexedDB)\n   - On reconnect: send queued edits, merge with server state using CRDT\n   - Deduplicate on reconnect (use operation ID)\n6. **Implement document title / metadata editing**\n   - Separate from content (metadata stored separately, not in CRDT history)\n   - `PATCH /documents/:id/metadata` (title, description)\n7. **V7 gate: End-to-end editing and offline support**\n   - Test: type in one client, see live update in another, disconnect, type locally, reconnect, verify edits synced and no conflicts\n   - Success: cursor latency p99 <300ms, zero edits lost on reconnect, merge conflicts auto-resolved\n\n**Rollback**: Feature flag `editor_v1_ui`; revert to old UI if needed.\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V7 passes (UI gate)\n- ✅ Internal user acceptance test (5 internal users, 1h session, no critical bugs)\n- ✅ Keyboard shortcuts (Ctrl+B for bold, etc.) documented\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 5: Document permissions and sharing**\n\n**What it unlocks**: Multi-user collaboration, access control.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 4 complete, V7 passes (UI is stable).\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Implement role-based access control (RBAC)**\n   - Roles: `owner`, `editor`, `viewer` (see Phase 2 design)\n   - Permission check on all doc operations: can user read this doc? edit?\n   - Endpoint: `GET /documents/:id/permissions` → list all users with access\n2. **Implement document sharing**\n   - `POST /documents/:id/share` → invite user by email or generate invite link\n   - Invite link: one-time use, expires in 7 days, grants specified role\n   - Permission notification: send email (if email system available; else skip for MVP)\n3. **Implement permission changes**\n   - Owner can change user role: `PATCH /documents/:id/permissions/:user_id` → change role to editor/viewer\n   - Owner can revoke access: `DELETE /documents/:id/permissions/:user_id`\n   - Audit log: record who changed permission, when\n4. **Implement real-time access control**\n   - If user's permission is revoked, server closes their WebSocket connection on next heartbeat\n   - Revoked user's local edits are discarded (not synced to server)\n5. **Implement document list filtering**\n   - `GET /documents` → list only docs user has access to (owner or shared with)\n   - Pagination: limit 50 per page\n6. **V8 gate: Authorization cannot be bypassed**\n   - Test: user A creates doc, shares with user B as viewer, user B attempts to edit → rejected\n   - Test: user B attempts to delete doc → rejected\n   - Test: user C (not invited) attempts to read doc → 403 Forbidden\n   - Success: 100% of unauthorized access attempts rejected, zero data leakage\n\n**Rollback**: Permission checks are additive (do not change existing doc schema); disable sharing during rollback (set `SHARING_ENABLED=false`).\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V8 passes (authorization gate)\n- ✅ Security test passed (permission boundary test, no leakage)\n- ✅ Audit log verified\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 6: History, versions, and audit trail**\n\n**What it unlocks**: Undo/redo across sessions, audit compliance.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 5 complete, V8 passes (permissions are enforced).\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Implement version snapshots**\n   - Every N operations (e.g., 1000) or every T time (e.g., 1h), store compressed doc state in `document_snapshots` table\n   - Snapshot includes: `doc_id, snapshot_id, op_count, state_compressed (gzip), created_at`\n   - Reduces replay overhead on large docs\n2. **Implement history browser (UI)**\n   - Show timeline of all edits: who, what, when\n   - Highlight changes (diff view)\n   - Filters: by user, by time range, by keyword\n3. **Implement revert**\n   - User can restore doc to any previous snapshot: `POST /documents/:id/restore?snapshot_id=...`\n   - Restore: load snapshot, discard all ops after snapshot, replay any user-requested edits\n   - Test: revert 10 edits ago, all subsequent edits still apply, no conflicts\n4. **Implement audit log**\n   - Every action logged: create doc, share doc, edit doc, delete doc, revert, permission change\n   - Log: `{ ts, user_id, action, doc_id, old_value, new_value }`\n   - Retention: keep for 2 years (ASSUMPTION; adjust based on compliance)\n5. **V9 gate: Restore correctness**\n   - Test: take 10 snapshots over 1h of editing, restore to snapshot #5, apply edits from #6–#10, verify no data loss and no conflicts\n   - Success: all edits reapply correctly, reconstructed state == state at snapshot #10\n\n**Rollback**: Snapshots are immutable (no deletes); rollback just disables restore UI.\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V9 passes (restore gate)\n- ✅ History queries fast (p99 <500ms)\n- ✅ Audit log verified\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 7: Scale and reliability (horizontal scaling, failover)**\n\n**What it unlocks**: 1000+ concurrent users per document, high availability.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 6 complete, V9 passes.\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Horizontal scaling**\n   - Deploy multiple server instances (N replicas)\n   - Load balancer (NGINX, ELB): distribute incoming WebSocket connections\n   - Sticky sessions: client connects to same server (via load balancer session affinity) to maintain state\n   - Alternative: Shared cache (Redis) for session state, any server can handle client\n2. **Document sharding (if needed)**\n   - If one document becomes too large (>1M edits), split operations across multiple servers\n   - Coordinate via message broker (e.g., Redis Pub/Sub or RabbitMQ)\n   - For MVP (Phase 7), assume single server per document is sufficient; defer sharding if V10 shows bottleneck\n3. **Database replication**\n   - Read replica: async replication for history queries (do not block writes on read-only queries)\n   - Write replica: if write latency becomes bottleneck, add dedicated write-optimized DB\n   - Transaction log: for crash recovery and consistency\n4. **Failover and health checks**\n   - Health check endpoint: `GET /health` → returns 200 if server is alive and DB reachable\n   - Load balancer removes unhealthy server (failed 3 health checks in a row)\n   - Client auto-reconnects to healthy server\n   - RTO: <10s (reconnect overhead)\n5. **Connection draining**\n   - On deploy: signal existing connections \"drain in progress\" (no new connections)\n   - Existing clients finish in-flight edits (timeout 30s)\n   - Close connection gracefully\n6. **V10 gate: SLO targets met at scale**\n   - Test: 1000 concurrent users on same document, continuous random edits, run 24h\n   - Measure: p50 latency <200ms, p99 <500ms, uptime 99.9%+, zero data loss, cost <$0.10/user/month\n   - Failover test: kill one server, verify clients reconnect within 10s, no downtime\n   - Success: all SLO targets met\n7. **Cost model validated**\n   - Server: 4 vCPU, 8GB RAM per instance × N = cost\n   - Database: storage + backup + replication = cost\n   - Network: data transfer out = cost\n   - Total cost per concurrent user confirmed <$0.10/month (or updated ASSUMPTION)\n\n**Rollback**: Canary deployment (10% users → monitor 24h → 50% → 100%); if issues arise, rollback to Phase 6 infrastructure.\n\n**Point of no return**: Migrate all users from Phase 4 (single-server) to Phase 7 (sharded); decommission old infrastructure. Gate: V10 passes + go/no-go approval from SRE.\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V10 passes (scale gate)\n- ✅ Cost per user validated\n- ✅ Failover tested and rehearsed\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 8: Security audit and public launch**\n\n**What it unlocks**: External users, public marketing.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 7 complete, V10 passes.\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Security audit (internal or external)**\n   - Code review: injection (SQL, JavaScript), XSS, CSRF, authentication bypass\n   - Dependency scan: `npm audit`, check for known CVEs\n   - Encryption audit: TLS 1.3, certificate management, key rotation\n   - Data handling audit: encryption at rest, retention, deletion, access logs\n   - V11 gate: zero critical/high CVEs, TLS 1.3 for all transport, encryption at rest, audit trail complete\n2. **Privacy policy and compliance**\n   - Document: what data is collected, how long retained, who can access\n   - If GDPR applies: DPA with any processor, data subject rights (access, delete), breach notification process\n   - If SOC2 applies: audit controls (access, change management, monitoring)\n   - If HIPAA applies: BAA with any processor, encryption, audit logging\n3. **Onboarding and documentation**\n   - User guide: how to create doc, invite users, use editor\n   - Admin guide: how to manage users, permissions, backups\n   - API documentation: if exposing API to partners\n4. **Support and escalation**\n   - Support email / chat\n   - Incident response playbook: what to do if system down, data corrupted, data breached\n   - On-call rotation: schedule SRE on-call, escalation path\n5. **Feature flag removal**\n   - Enable for all users (remove `editor_v1` flag)\n   - Public documentation live\n6. **V11 gate: Security audit passes**\n   - Test: penetration test (attempt injection, XSS, CSRF, auth bypass), result: all attacks blocked\n   - Success: zero critical/high vulns, privacy policy matches data handling, audit controls in place\n\n**Rollback**: Keep internal-only flag available; if critical issue found, re-enable flag to limit exposure.\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V11 passes (security gate)\n- ✅ Team ready for on-call rotation\n- ✅ Incident playbook tested\n\n---\n\n## 6. Let me build the plan for the **Real-Time Collaborative Document Editor**.\n\n---\n\n# BUILD PLAN: Real-Time Collaborative Document Editor\n\n## 1. Classification and constraints\n\n**What is being built**: A real-time collaborative document editor (synchronous multi-user editing, e.g., Google Docs / Notion style).\n\n**Type**: Software (distributed system: frontend + backend + database).\n\n**Greenfield or brownfield**: Greenfield (new system).\n\n**Scale**: Assumed not-small. Collaborative editors face high concurrency: typical assumption is 100–1000 concurrent users, documents with 10k–1M characters, 10–100 edits per second under peak. Conflict resolution and network partition handling are mandatory.\n\n**Dominant constraint**: **Correctness > Availability > Latency > Cost**. Concurrent edits must merge without data loss; network partitions and crashes must not corrupt the document. Latency matters (users expect <200ms end-to-end), but data integrity is non-negotiable.\n\n**Worst failure**: Data corruption or loss during concurrent edits; or unauthorized access to private documents.\n\n**Budgets that matter** (labeled):\n- **Latency**: p99 keystroke-to-render <200ms (ASSUMPTION: derived from user expectations, not stated in request; confirm with product owner).\n- **Throughput**: 50+ concurrent users per document (ASSUMPTION: typical collaborative editing; spike test required).\n- **Availability**: 99.5% uptime (ASSUMPTION: not a safety-critical system, so 99.5% is defensible; SaaS standard is 99.9%, but cost differs).\n- **Storage**: Full edit history, no limit (ASSUMPTION: unlimited history; cost model TBD in Phase 3).\n- **Infrastructure cost**: <$0.10/concurrent-user/month (ASSUMPTION: competitive with existing players; validate in V10).\n- **RTO (Recovery Time Objective)**: 1 hour (ASSUMPTION: acceptable for non-critical tool).\n- **RPO (Recovery Point Objective)**: 15 minutes (ASSUMPTION: lose ≤15min of edits on total failure).\n\n**Missing inputs that would change the plan**:\n1. **Real scale requirements** (how many users, documents, concurrent edits?): Supplied by product owner before Phase 1. If >10k concurrent users, sharding strategy changes (Phase 7).\n2. **Data residency / compliance** (GDPR, SOC2, HIPAA?): Supplied by legal/product before Phase 2. If HIPAA, Phase 2 adds encryption design; Phase 5 adds audit logging.\n3. **Team skill and size**: Assumed 4–6 engineers (full-stack capable). If smaller, Phase 0–1 may merge; if larger, parallel tracks possible.\n4. **Timeline**: Not stated. Assumed 6–9 months to Phase 8 (public launch); if compressed to 3 months, methodolgy exceptions E1 (spike before skeleton) and E2 (parallel tracks) apply.\n\n**Assumption: no existing system to integrate**. If there is a legacy document store or auth system, this becomes brownfield (Phase 0 runs behind a flag, Phase 5 does cutover). Plan assumes greenfield unless told otherwise.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Dependency map\n\n**Structural**:\n- **Conflict-resolution algorithm** blocks all downstream editor code. Choice of CRDT vs OT is irreversible once data is in the database (R3).\n- **API contract** (WebSocket message schema, REST endpoints) blocks client and server implementation.\n- **Document data model** (schema for users, docs, operations, versions) blocks persistence and recovery.\n- **Authentication scheme** (JWT, OAuth, sessions) blocks authorization and audit logging.\n\n**Runtime**:\n- **Database** must be running before server can persist edits.\n- **Real-time transport** (WebSocket) must be deployed before clients can sync live.\n- **Identity provider** (if external, e.g., Okta) must be up before production login.\n\n**Decision** (hard-to-reverse tradeoffs):\n- **CRDT vs Operational Transform (OT)**: Affects merge semantics, offline support, latency. R3.\n- **Self-hosted vs managed PaaS** (Firebase, Firestore, Replicache): Affects data sovereignty, cost predictability, operational burden. R3.\n- **Document versioning**: Full history vs snapshots + deltas. R3 (storage model, query patterns).\n- **Consistency model**: Strong (Raft consensus) vs eventual (CRDT + async). R3 (user experience, conflict handling).\n- **Monolith vs microservices**: R3 if public API will be exposed; but can defer to Phase 7 if not.\n\n**Validation**:\n- **CRDT merge correctness** must be proven before real documents exist (V1).\n- **Latency under load** must hit SLO before scaling to many users (V6).\n- **No data loss under network partition** must be tested (V9 on chaos).\n- **Authorization gates** must be audited before multi-user sharing (V8).\n\n**Risk / Security**:\n- **Authentication** must guard document access (risk: unauthorized read/write) — must pass V5 before Phase 3 real documents.\n- **Authorization checks** (per-document permissions) must guard exposure — must pass V8 before Phase 5 sharing.\n- **Transport encryption (TLS)** must be in place before real user data. Baseline in Phase 0 (internal), hardened in Phase 7.\n- **Data isolation** (one user cannot see another's docs) must be enforced before public launch (V11).\n\n**Organizational**:\n- **Product owner decision** on scale / compliance requirements: needed before Phase 1 (lead time 1–2 weeks if not ready).\n- **Security review** (internal or external audit): needed before Phase 8 public launch; lead time 4–6 weeks.\n- **DevOps / SRE** readiness (infrastructure, observability, on-call): needed for Phase 7 production scale.\n\n**Economic**:\n- **Real-time infrastructure cost model** (V2 operational spike): needed before committing to self-hosted (vs PaaS flip).\n- **Storage cost** (full history vs snapshots): needed before scaling beyond 100k docs (V3 storage spike).\n\n**No instances**:\n- No brownfield system (greenfield assumed; revised if legacy integration discovered).\n- No AI / agentic layer (no LLM, no guardrails, no orchestration).\n\n---\n\n## 3. Tradeoff gates\n\n| Decision | Reversibility | Default | Assumption | Validated by | Flip condition |\n|----------|---|---------|-----------|--------------|---|\n| **CRDT vs Operational Transform** | R3 (data migration, user experience break) | **CRDT** (Yjs library) — simpler merge semantics, offline-first, industry trend | CRDT performance is acceptable (<500ms latency under conflict storms); team can operate Yjs correctly | **V1**: Spike 2-user Yjs editor, 50 concurrent edits, p99 latency <500ms, zero data loss | V1 fails (p99 >1s or complexity >2 weeks) → switch to proven OT library (ShareDB) |\n| **Self-hosted vs managed PaaS** | R3 (vendor lock-in, data migration, cost structure) | **Self-hosted** (Node.js + WebSocket + PostgreSQL) — control, compliance flexibility, cost predictable at scale | Team can operate real-time infrastructure; compliance does not mandate external provider; cost per user <$0.10 at 1k users | **V2**: 1-week staging canary (50-user synthetic load), measure uptime ≥99%, cost model identified, on-call response <15min | V2 fails (uptime <99% or cost model >$0.15/user) → flip to Firebase Realtime DB or Replicache + managed backend |\n| **Full history vs snapshots + deltas** | R3 (affects audit trail, storage model, undo/redo) | **Full CRDT history** (every edit operation stored immutably) — enables infinite undo, audit trail, compliance | Storage cost acceptable (<$1/doc/month); DB query latency acceptable (<2s for \"show history\") | **V3**: Simulate 3-month doc lifecycle (insert/delete/format ops), measure DB size, query p99 latency for \"get history\", cost/doc | V3 fails (cost >$1/doc or query p99 >2s) → flip to periodic snapshots + delta compression |\n| **Consistency model** | R3 (affects conflict UX, network partition handling) | **Eventual consistency** (CRDT merge, no blocking) — offline support, always available, auto-resolve conflicts | Users tolerate 100–500ms lag between clients; automatic merges produce sensible results (no invisible data loss) | **V4**: Conflict scenario test (100 realistic patterns: concurrent insert/delete, format conflicts), reconstruct doc, zero data loss, merge quality acceptable | V4 fails (data loss or merge quality poor) → add Raft consensus layer for strong consistency (slower, blocks offline) |\n| **WebSocket vs polling** | R2 (deployment, performance) | **WebSocket** (bidirectional, low latency, persistent connection) — industry standard for real-time | Infrastructure supports persistent connections; firewalls permit WebSocket upgrade (fallback to polling if not) | Cheap check: load test 10k concurrent WS connections, measure memory/CPU, latency p99 <200ms | Load test fails (memory >500MB/1k conn or latency >500ms) → add polling fallback, prioritize WS |\n\n**R1 decisions** (one line):\n- Use TypeScript for type safety and shared schemas between client/server. Use Docker + Kubernetes for reproducibility and scaling.\n\n**N/A**:\n- **Build vs buy for CRDT**: Not a tradeoff. Yjs is proven, open-source, industry standard; building from scratch is not viable.\n- **Monolith vs microservices**: Deferred to Phase 7 (if public API needed); greenfield monolith is simpler (decouple later if needed).\n\n---\n\n## 4. Walking skeleton (Phase 0)\n\n**Who can reach it**: Internal developers and QA only. Behind feature flag `editor_v1`. No real user data. No production database access until V0 passes.\n\n**One real request end-to-end**:\n1. **Client**: Open browser, navigate to `http://localhost:3000/editor?doc_id=test123`\n2. **Client**: JavaScript initiates WebSocket upgrade to `ws://localhost:3001/sync`\n3. **Server**: Receives upgrade, creates session, verifies internal (no auth yet, but request is from internal IP)\n4. **Server**: Fetches doc state from database (initial CRDT state = empty)\n5. **Server**: Sends initial state to client: `{ type: \"init\", state: {...} }`\n6. **Client**: Receives state, initializes local CRDT, renders empty editor\n7. **User**: Types \"hello world\" (6 keystrokes)\n8. **Client**: For each keystroke, generates CRDT insert operation, sends to server: `{ type: \"update\", op: { pos: X, content: \"c\" } }`\n9. **Server**: Receives update, applies to document CRDT, broadcasts to all connected clients (including sender), persists to `document_updates` table\n10. **All clients**: Receive broadcast, apply op to local CRDT, re-render (should show \"hello world\" live)\n11. **Server**: Logs trace: `session:open → update:received → crdt:apply → db:write → broadcast:sent`\n12. **Latency**: <100ms local (keystroke to own client render), <200ms remote (keystroke to other client render)\n\n**Deployment**:\n- Docker Compose (`docker-compose.yml`):\n  - `server` (Node.js + Express + `ws` library + Yjs)\n  - `db` (PostgreSQL 14)\n  - `adminer` (for local DB inspection)\n- GitHub Actions pipeline:\n  1. `npm ci` (install exact versions)\n  2. `npm test` (unit tests)\n  3. `docker build` (server image)\n  4. `docker push` (to ECR, or local registry for local CI)\n  5. Deploy to `localhost:3000` (for local dev) or ECS/K8s cluster (for staging)\n\n**Logging (structured, JSON)**:\n- Session lifecycle: `{ ts, event: \"session:open\", session_id, user_id:\"internal\", doc_id }`\n- Update received: `{ ts, event: \"update:received\", op_count, op_size_bytes }`\n- CRDT apply: `{ ts, event: \"crdt:apply\", op_id, latency_ms, merge_conflict: false }`\n- DB write: `{ ts, event: \"db:write\", latency_ms, table: \"document_updates\" }`\n- Broadcast: `{ ts, event: \"broadcast:sent\", client_count, latency_ms }`\n- Errors: `{ ts, level: \"error\", component, message, stack_trace }`\n\n**Monitoring (dashboards in CloudWatch / Datadog)**:\n- **Latency histogram**: keystroke → server receive (network) + server apply (CRDT) + server broadcast (network) + client render. P50, P95, P99.\n- **WebSocket status**: active connections, disconnected, reconnect rate.\n- **Database metrics**: write latency (p99), connection pool utilization.\n- **CRDT metrics**: conflict rate (per-minute), merge operation count, state size growth over time.\n- **Memory / CPU**: per-connection baseline (should be <1MB/conn for skeleton).\n- **Errors**: exception rate, error type breakdown.\n\n**Rollback**:\n1. Feature flag `editor_v1` set to false → all requests route to old (or no-op) editor.\n2. Database schema rolled back (migration reversible; schema only adds `document_updates` table, does not alter existing).\n3. Docker image reverted: `docker pull old_image_sha` → re-deploy.\n4. Verification: old clients continue to work; no data loss.\n\n**Exit check V0** (all must pass):\n1. ✅ **Real request succeeds**: User creates doc, types 50 characters in one client, second client sees live update within 200ms, first client sees reflection of their own edit.\n2. ✅ **Deploy repeatable**: Run full pipeline 3 times in a row; each deploy succeeds, starts, and old clients still work.\n3. ✅ **Rollback succeeds**: Rollback to previous image, verify system still responds; re-deploy, system comes back.\n4. ✅ **Injected failure alerts**: Kill server process; alert fires in CloudWatch within 30 seconds.\n5. ✅ **Baselines recorded**: Latency histogram (p50, p99) at 1 user / 1 doc; memory baseline (~50MB).\n\n---\n\n## 5. Phases\n\n### **Phase 1: Conflict resolution and data model validation**\n\n**What it unlocks**: Confirmed merge strategy; finalized document schema; unblocked build of core logic.\n\n**Depends on**: V0 passes (skeleton is stable); product owner confirms scale targets.\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **V1 spike: CRDT correctness under load**\n   - Implement toy 2-user Yjs editor (React + Yjs, no server)\n   - Generate 50 random concurrent edits (inserts, deletes, concurrent at same position)\n   - Measure: latency p99, operation count before/after merge, data loss incidents\n   - Success: p99 <500ms, zero data loss\n2. **V2 spike: Operational readiness**\n   - Deploy Phase 0 skeleton to staging with 1-week continuous 50-user synthetic load (bots editing same doc continuously)\n   - Measure: uptime (target 99%+), infrastructure cost ($/user/month), on-call response time (<15min)\n   - Document: cost model, scaling bottlenecks (CPU, memory, DB), connection lifecycle\n3. **V3 spike: Storage and query cost**\n   - Simulate 3-month document lifecycle: 10k edits per doc, varied sizes (1KB–1MB final size)\n   - Measure: DB table size, query latency for \"get all edits for doc\", cost per doc at various scales\n   - Success: cost <$1/doc/month, query p99 <2s\n4. **V4 spike: Conflict merge quality**\n   - Generate 100 realistic conflict scenarios (concurrent insert/delete/format at overlapping positions)\n   - Reconstruct doc from CRDT operations, verify: zero data loss, no unintended duplicates, merge is deterministic\n   - Success: zero data loss, merge result matches expected\n5. **Document data model (schema design)**\n   - `users` table: `id, email, created_at`\n   - `documents` table: `id, owner_id, title, created_at, updated_at`\n   - `document_updates` table: `id, doc_id, op_id (ULID), client_id, op (JSON CRDT op), created_at, applied_at` — immutable append-only log\n   - `sessions` table: `id, user_id, token_hash, created_at, expires_at`\n   - Indexes: `(doc_id, op_id)` for fast replay, `(user_id, created_at)` for user's docs\n6. **API contract sketch**\n   - `POST /documents` → create doc, return `{ id, state }`\n   - `GET /documents/:id` → fetch doc state (reconstructed from ops)\n   - `WebSocket /sync` → persistent connection for real-time updates; messages: `{ type: \"update\", op }, { type: \"init\", state }`\n\n**Rollback**: All spikes are throw-away code. If V1–V4 fail, go back to step 3 (Tradeoff gates) and re-plan with different defaults (e.g., OT instead of CRDT, Firebase instead of self-hosted).\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V1, V2, V3, V4 all pass (or flip condition triggered and re-plan complete)\n- ✅ Data model reviewed and accepted\n- ✅ API sketch documented\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 2: Authentication and authorization blueprint**\n\n**What it unlocks**: Spec for auth system; permission model; ready to build hardened implementation.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 1 complete; product owner confirms compliance requirements (GDPR, SOC2, etc.).\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Design auth system**\n   - **Token strategy**: JWT (stateless, scalable) vs sessions (stateful, simpler)\n   - **Default**: JWT with 15min access token + 7-day refresh token (refresh allows long sessions without leaking access token)\n   - **Refresh token rotation**: New refresh token issued with each refresh; old token invalidated\n   - **Implementation**: Node.js `jsonwebtoken` library, RS256 (asymmetric signing for scalability), keys in environment\n2. **Design permission model**\n   - **Roles**: `owner`, `editor`, `viewer`, `commenter` (commenter deferred to Phase 5)\n   - **Owner**: create doc, delete doc, change permissions, edit content\n   - **Editor**: edit content, view edit history\n   - **Viewer**: read-only, cannot edit\n   - **Storage**: `document_permissions` table: `doc_id, user_id, role, granted_by, granted_at`\n3. **Design WebSocket auth**\n   - Client sends JWT in WebSocket upgrade header: `Upgrade: websocket, Authorization: Bearer <token>`\n   - Server verifies token on upgrade; rejects if invalid or expired\n   - Periodic token refresh: client sends refresh token, server issues new access token (without reconnect needed)\n4. **V5 gate: Authentication cannot be bypassed**\n   - Test: attempt WebSocket connect without token → rejected 401\n   - Test: attempt with expired token → rejected 401\n   - Test: attempt to call `POST /update` (if added) without token → rejected 401\n   - Test: with valid token, call succeeds\n   - Success: 100% of unauthenticated requests rejected, zero data leakage\n5. **Password policy and reset**\n   - Passwords hashed with bcrypt (cost factor 12)\n   - Password reset via email link (expires in 1h, single-use)\n   - No plain-text passwords in logs\n\n**Rollback**: Auth logic is in middleware; disable entire auth layer during Phase 2 by setting `SKIP_AUTH=true` in config (internal use only).\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V5 passes (auth gate)\n- ✅ Auth design document reviewed and approved\n- ✅ Permission matrix tested in unit tests\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 3: Core editor backend (CRDT sync, database persistence)**\n\n**What it unlocks**: Real documents, production data, live collaboration.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 2 complete, V5 passes (auth is in place).\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Implement CRDT state machine**\n   - Integrate Yjs library: `const ydoc = new Y.Doc(), ytext = ydoc.getText(\"content\")`\n   - Track operation sequence: each insert/delete/format generates CRDT operation\n   - Implement state reconstruction: given all historical ops, replay and reconstruct doc state\n   - Test: 5 concurrent clients, 200 edits each (1000 total), verify all clients converge to same state\n2. **Implement WebSocket server**\n   - Use `ws` library (Node.js)\n   - On client connect: authenticate token, load doc state from DB, send initial state\n   - On message receive: apply to CRDT, broadcast to all clients connected to this doc, persist to DB\n   - Heartbeat every 30s (keep-alive)\n   - On disconnect: clean up session, mark client as offline (but keep doc state)\n3. **Implement database persistence**\n   - Persist every update: `INSERT INTO document_updates (doc_id, op_id, op, created_at) VALUES (...)`\n   - Bulk inserts (batch every 10 ops or 100ms) for efficiency\n   - Transaction: apply op to CRDT + insert to DB atomically (serializable isolation)\n4. **Implement document fetch (reconstruct from history)**\n   - `GET /documents/:id/state`: SELECT all ops from `document_updates` WHERE doc_id = :id, replay in order, return final state\n   - Cache final state as snapshot (if V3 spike confirmed snapshots needed) to avoid full replay on every fetch\n5. **Implement rate-limiting**\n   - Per-user: max 10 edits/second (prevent abuse)\n   - Per-document: max 100 edits/second (sanity check)\n6. **V6 gate: Correctness and latency under load**\n   - Test: 5 concurrent clients, each sends 200 random edits, measure end-to-end latency (keystroke to all clients render), run for 10 min\n   - Check: zero data loss (reconstruct all clients' state, verify identical), zero crashes, p99 latency <200ms\n   - Success: p50 <100ms, p99 <200ms, zero data loss\n\n**Rollback**: Database migration is reversible (new table only); revert code, migration automatically rolls back on redeploy.\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V6 passes (performance gate)\n- ✅ Stress test passed (chaos test: kill server mid-sync, restart, verify recovery)\n- ✅ Latency baselines recorded\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 4: Client-side editor UI (end-to-end real-time editing)**\n\n**What it unlocks**: User-facing editor experience, internal user acceptance testing.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 3 complete, V6 passes (backend is stable and fast).\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Implement React editor component**\n   - Use CodeMirror or ProseMirror (proven editors with real-time support)\n   - Integrate Yjs: `const binding = new CodemirrorBinding(ytext, cm.view, new Set([cm, provider]))`\n   - Two-way sync: local edits → Yjs → WebSocket → server → other clients\n2. **Implement WebSocket client**\n   - Auto-reconnect with exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s, ..., max 30s)\n   - Offline queue: if connection drops, queue edits locally, replay on reconnect\n   - Conflict resolution: CRDT handles automatically, no user intervention needed\n3. **Implement cursor presence (show other users' cursors)**\n   - Broadcast cursor position on keystroke / mouse click (throttled, max 1 per 100ms)\n   - Subscribe to other users' cursors, draw in UI (e.g., color-coded carets)\n   - V7 gate: cursor latency p99 <300ms, smooth visual experience\n4. **Implement local undo/redo**\n   - Yjs undo manager: `const uiManager = new UndoManager([ytext])`\n   - Undo respects CRDT semantics: undo an insert, even if other user deleted later chars\n5. **Implement offline support**\n   - Client queues edits while offline (in IndexedDB)\n   - On reconnect: send queued edits, merge with server state using CRDT\n   - Deduplicate on reconnect (use operation ID)\n6. **Implement document title / metadata editing**\n   - Separate from content (metadata stored separately, not in CRDT history)\n   - `PATCH /documents/:id/metadata` (title, description)\n7. **V7 gate: End-to-end editing and offline support**\n   - Test: type in one client, see live update in another, disconnect, type locally, reconnect, verify edits synced and no conflicts\n   - Success: cursor latency p99 <300ms, zero edits lost on reconnect, merge conflicts auto-resolved\n\n**Rollback**: Feature flag `editor_v1_ui`; revert to old UI if needed.\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V7 passes (UI gate)\n- ✅ Internal user acceptance test (5 internal users, 1h session, no critical bugs)\n- ✅ Keyboard shortcuts (Ctrl+B for bold, etc.) documented\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 5: Document permissions and sharing**\n\n**What it unlocks**: Multi-user collaboration, access control.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 4 complete, V7 passes (UI is stable).\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Implement role-based access control (RBAC)**\n   - Roles: `owner`, `editor`, `viewer` (see Phase 2 design)\n   - Permission check on all doc operations: can user read this doc? edit?\n   - Endpoint: `GET /documents/:id/permissions` → list all users with access\n2. **Implement document sharing**\n   - `POST /documents/:id/share` → invite user by email or generate invite link\n   - Invite link: one-time use, expires in 7 days, grants specified role\n   - Permission notification: send email (if email system available; else skip for MVP)\n3. **Implement permission changes**\n   - Owner can change user role: `PATCH /documents/:id/permissions/:user_id` → change role to editor/viewer\n   - Owner can revoke access: `DELETE /documents/:id/permissions/:user_id`\n   - Audit log: record who changed permission, when\n4. **Implement real-time access control**\n   - If user's permission is revoked, server closes their WebSocket connection on next heartbeat\n   - Revoked user's local edits are discarded (not synced to server)\n5. **Implement document list filtering**\n   - `GET /documents` → list only docs user has access to (owner or shared with)\n   - Pagination: limit 50 per page\n6. **V8 gate: Authorization cannot be bypassed**\n   - Test: user A creates doc, shares with user B as viewer, user B attempts to edit → rejected\n   - Test: user B attempts to delete doc → rejected\n   - Test: user C (not invited) attempts to read doc → 403 Forbidden\n   - Success: 100% of unauthorized access attempts rejected, zero data leakage\n\n**Rollback**: Permission checks are additive (do not change existing doc schema); disable sharing during rollback (set `SHARING_ENABLED=false`).\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V8 passes (authorization gate)\n- ✅ Security test passed (permission boundary test, no leakage)\n- ✅ Audit log verified\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 6: History, versions, and audit trail**\n\n**What it unlocks**: Undo/redo across sessions, audit compliance.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 5 complete, V8 passes (permissions are enforced).\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Implement version snapshots**\n   - Every N operations (e.g., 1000) or every T time (e.g., 1h), store compressed doc state in `document_snapshots` table\n   - Snapshot includes: `doc_id, snapshot_id, op_count, state_compressed (gzip), created_at`\n   - Reduces replay overhead on large docs\n2. **Implement history browser (UI)**\n   - Show timeline of all edits: who, what, when\n   - Highlight changes (diff view)\n   - Filters: by user, by time range, by keyword\n3. **Implement revert**\n   - User can restore doc to any previous snapshot: `POST /documents/:id/restore?snapshot_id=...`\n   - Restore: load snapshot, discard all ops after snapshot, replay any user-requested edits\n   - Test: revert 10 edits ago, all subsequent edits still apply, no conflicts\n4. **Implement audit log**\n   - Every action logged: create doc, share doc, edit doc, delete doc, revert, permission change\n   - Log: `{ ts, user_id, action, doc_id, old_value, new_value }`\n   - Retention: keep for 2 years (ASSUMPTION; adjust based on compliance)\n5. **V9 gate: Restore correctness**\n   - Test: take 10 snapshots over 1h of editing, restore to snapshot #5, apply edits from #6–#10, verify no data loss and no conflicts\n   - Success: all edits reapply correctly, reconstructed state == state at snapshot #10\n\n**Rollback**: Snapshots are immutable (no deletes); rollback just disables restore UI.\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V9 passes (restore gate)\n- ✅ History queries fast (p99 <500ms)\n- ✅ Audit log verified\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 7: Scale and reliability (horizontal scaling, failover)**\n\n**What it unlocks**: 1000+ concurrent users per document, high availability.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 6 complete, V9 passes.\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Horizontal scaling**\n   - Deploy multiple server instances (N replicas)\n   - Load balancer (NGINX, ELB): distribute incoming WebSocket connections\n   - Sticky sessions: client connects to same server (via load balancer session affinity) to maintain state\n   - Alternative: Shared cache (Redis) for session state, any server can handle client\n2. **Document sharding (if needed)**\n   - If one document becomes too large (>1M edits), split operations across multiple servers\n   - Coordinate via message broker (e.g., Redis Pub/Sub or RabbitMQ)\n   - For MVP (Phase 7), assume single server per document is sufficient; defer sharding if V10 shows bottleneck\n3. **Database replication**\n   - Read replica: async replication for history queries (do not block writes on read-only queries)\n   - Write replica: if write latency becomes bottleneck, add dedicated write-optimized DB\n   - Transaction log: for crash recovery and consistency\n4. **Failover and health checks**\n   - Health check endpoint: `GET /health` → returns 200 if server is alive and DB reachable\n   - Load balancer removes unhealthy server (failed 3 health checks in a row)\n   - Client auto-reconnects to healthy server\n   - RTO: <10s (reconnect overhead)\n5. **Connection draining**\n   - On deploy: signal existing connections \"drain in progress\" (no new connections)\n   - Existing clients finish in-flight edits (timeout 30s)\n   - Close connection gracefully\n6. **V10 gate: SLO targets met at scale**\n   - Test: 1000 concurrent users on same document, continuous random edits, run 24h\n   - Measure: p50 latency <200ms, p99 <500ms, uptime 99.9%+, zero data loss, cost <$0.10/user/month\n   - Failover test: kill one server, verify clients reconnect within 10s, no downtime\n   - Success: all SLO targets met\n7. **Cost model validated**\n   - Server: 4 vCPU, 8GB RAM per instance × N = cost\n   - Database: storage + backup + replication = cost\n   - Network: data transfer out = cost\n   - Total cost per concurrent user confirmed <$0.10/month (or updated ASSUMPTION)\n\n**Rollback**: Canary deployment (10% users → monitor 24h → 50% → 100%); if issues arise, rollback to Phase 6 infrastructure.\n\n**Point of no return**: Migrate all users from Phase 4 (single-server) to Phase 7 (sharded); decommission old infrastructure. Gate: V10 passes + go/no-go approval from SRE.\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V10 passes (scale gate)\n- ✅ Cost per user validated\n- ✅ Failover tested and rehearsed\n\n---\n\n### **Phase 8: Security audit and public launch**\n\n**What it unlocks**: External users, public marketing.\n\n**Depends on**: Phase 7 complete, V10 passes.\n\n**Tasks**:\n1. **Security audit (internal or external)**\n   - Code review: injection (SQL, JavaScript), XSS, CSRF, authentication bypass\n   - Dependency scan: `npm audit`, check for known CVEs\n   - Encryption audit: TLS 1.3, certificate management, key rotation\n   - Data handling audit: encryption at rest, retention, deletion, access logs\n   - V11 gate: zero critical/high CVEs, TLS 1.3 for all transport, encryption at rest, audit trail complete\n2. **Privacy policy and compliance**\n   - Document: what data is collected, how long retained, who can access\n   - If GDPR applies: DPA with any processor, data subject rights (access, delete), breach notification process\n   - If SOC2 applies: audit controls (access, change management, monitoring)\n   - If HIPAA applies: BAA with any processor, encryption, audit logging\n3. **Onboarding and documentation**\n   - User guide: how to create doc, invite users, use editor\n   - Admin guide: how to manage users, permissions, backups\n   - API documentation: if exposing API to partners\n4. **Support and escalation**\n   - Support email / chat\n   - Incident response playbook: what to do if system down, data corrupted, data breached\n   - On-call rotation: schedule SRE on-call, escalation path\n5. **Feature flag removal**\n   - Enable for all users (remove `editor_v1` flag)\n   - Public documentation live\n6. **V11 gate: Security audit passes**\n   - Test: penetration test (attempt injection, XSS, CSRF, auth bypass), result: all attacks blocked\n   - Success: zero critical/high vulns, privacy policy matches data handling, audit controls in place\n\n**Rollback**: Keep internal-only flag available; if critical issue found, re-enable flag to limit exposure.\n\n**Exit check**:\n- ✅ V11 passes (security gate)\n- ✅ Team ready for on-call rotation\n- ✅ Incident playbook tested\n\n---\n\n## 6. Cross-cutting concerns\n\n| Concern | Phase 0 | Phase 1 | Phase 2 | Phase 3 | Phase 4 | Phase 5 | Phase 6 | Phase 7 | Phase 8 |\n|---------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|---------|\n| **Security** | Internal-only skeleton, no real data, feature flag hides from users. No auth yet. | CRDT algorithm reviewed for side-channel attacks (timing, state disclosure). | Implement auth (JWT RS256, refresh rotation, HTTPS everywhere). Secrets in env vars, rotate keys monthly. | Input validation on all CRDT ops (bounds, type checks). Rate-limit per-user (10 edits/s). Log failed validation. | CSP headers (no inline script). Sanitize HTML in editor (prevent XSS). DOMPurify for user content. | Permission matrix in DB, every read/write audited. Audit log immutable (append-only). | History queries filtered by permission (user cannot see other users' edits). Snapshot access gated by permission. | TLS 1.3 for all connections (WebSocket + HTTP). Certificate pinning optional. API rate-limit at load balancer (100 req/s per IP). | Full security audit (code review, pen test, dependency audit). Privacy policy enforcement (data retention, deletion, DPA). GDPR consent on signup if needed. |\n| **Observability** | Structured JSON logs: session lifecycle, updates (received/applied/persisted), errors. CloudWatch dashboard: latency histogram (p50/p99), WebSocket status, DB latency. Baseline metrics at 1-user load. | Add CRDT-level metrics: conflict rate (per-min), merge op count, state size growth (MB/hour). Flamegraph tools for latency spikes (e.g., node --prof). | Auth metrics: login success/fail rate, token refresh rate, invalid token attempts (alert if spike). Dashboard for auth latency. | DB query logs (slow query alert >100ms). Index usage audit. Replication lag if applicable. Operation audit trail (every update logged with timestamp, user, op). | Frontend RUM (Real User Monitoring): keystroke-to-render latency, frame rate, error rate (Sentry integration). Heatmaps of feature usage. | Permission access logs (every read/write check, with user/doc/result). Alert on unusual patterns (bulk reads, access spikes). Audit trail query latency. | History query latency (p50/p99), snapshot size growth (alert if >1GB), restore operation logs. | Distributed tracing (Jaeger / Datadog): request flows across load balancer → server → DB. Connection pool exhaustion alerts. Per-server metrics (CPU, memory, connections). | SLO dashboard (uptime, latency, error rate, cost). Customer-facing status page. Incident log (time to detect, time to resolve). Monthly metrics review. |\n| **Reproducibility** | Docker Compose for local dev; seeded test data (`seed.sql`); all config in version control (no hardcoded secrets). CI/CD pipeline in GitHub Actions (build → test → push → deploy). | Spike code tagged in git (reference only, not prod). Config versioning (all settings in version control). | API schema versioned (Swagger/OpenAPI 3.0); database migration scripts (Flyway/Liquibase, reversible); backward compat tests. | DB fixtures: standard docs with known edit history (used for performance testing). Chaos tests with fixed seed (reproducible). Snapshot & restore tested in CI. | Component tests (mock WebSocket server), snapshot tests (React), E2E tests in docker-compose. UI regression tests (visual diff). | Permission matrix fixtures (test scenarios). Test coverage >80% for auth/permission code. | History fixtures (100-edit doc with known snapshots). Restore tests in CI. | Docker image SHAs tagged; Kubernetes manifests in git; Terraform/Helm for infra-as-code. Configuration per environment (dev, staging, prod). | Release tagging (v1.0.0, v1.0.1, ...). Release notes generated from commits. Rollback playbook tested monthly. Brownfield migration docs (if applying to existing system later). |\n| **Resilience** | WebSocket heartbeat every 30s (keep-alive). Exponential backoff for reconnect (1s, 2s, 4s, ..., max 30s). Timeout on ping = 60s (reconnect after timeout). | Chaos test: kill CRDT process mid-merge, restart, verify recovery (no state corruption). | Session timeout 1h inactivity; nightly cleanup of stale sessions. Password reset token 1-use, 1h expiry. | DB replication (read replica); write replica if write latency becomes bottleneck. Transaction logs for crash recovery. Immutable operation log (append-only, no deletes). Snapshot backups nightly. | Client-side queue for offline edits (IndexedDB, up to 1000 ops); deduplicate on reconnect. Retry logic: exponential backoff up to 10 attempts. | Permission cache (Redis TTL 5min); fallback to DB if cache miss. Backup permission matrix nightly (separate DB snapshot). | Snapshot immutability (no deletes); append-only history. Backup snapshots nightly (separate S3 bucket). Point-in-time recovery tested monthly. | Multi-zone / multi-region deployment (if needed for high availability); failover <10s. Connection draining on shutdown (30s graceful close). Health checks every 5s. Canary deployment (10% → 50% → 100%). | Customer SLA: RTO 1h, RPO 15min. Incident runbook (what to do if down, data corrupted, breached). On-call escalation (L1 → L2 → L3). Daily backup verification. Disaster recovery test quarterly. |\n\n---\n\n## 8. AI layer\n\n**N/A — no AI component.** This system does not involve LLMs, RAG, agentic behavior, or guardrails. Methodology exceptions\n\n**None.** The canonical method (walking skeleton in Phase 0, validation gates before exposure, security before feature completeness) applies directly without exception.\n\nThe skeleton runs in Phase 0 with all appropriate controls (internal-only, feature flag, no real data). Scale and security are validated in phases 1–7 before public launch in Phase 8. No need to bypass the skeleton or defer validation.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Deliberately deferred\n\nEach deferred item names the condition that pulls it forward:\n\n1. **Comments and annotations** — Deferred until Phase 5 exit check passes (multi-user sharing required; no point in comments on single-user doc).\n2. **Advanced formatting (tables, code blocks, embedded media)** — Deferred until Phase 4 exit check passes (foundational editor proven first).\n3. **Full-text search across document library** — Deferred until Phase 6 exit check passes (requires indexed history; expensive to build before history layer solid).\n4. **Real-time notifications (email, Slack notifications on edits)** — Deferred until Phase 7 exit check passes (requires stable real-time layer; can trigger notification storms if unreliable).\n5. **Document templates and quick-start wizard** — Deferred until Phase 4 exit check passes (UI maturity required; can add before Phase 5 if UX testing warrants).\n6. **AI-powered suggestions (grammar, tone, auto-complete)** — Deferred until Phase 8 + cost budget approved (requires LLM API integration, introduces cost and latency dependencies; pull forward if business case emerges).\n7. **Public API for integrations (Zapier, GitHub, Slack)** — Deferred until Phase 8 exit check passes (requires stable versioned API, security audit on external data; can add after public launch).\n8. **Offline-first PWA (installable web app)** — Deferred until Phase 7 exit check passes (depends on stable offline sync; add if market demand validated).\n9. **Mobile apps (iOS, Android)** — Deferred until Phase 8 exit check passes + native app business case validated (major engineering effort; Phase 8 proves web MVP first).\n10. **Document federation (docs across organizations, org-to-org access control)** — Deferred until Phase 8 + organizational requirements clarified (adds identity complexity; defer until real demand).\n\n---\n\n## Self-check\n\n✅ **Phase 0 is a production walking skeleton**: User creates doc, types, sees live update in second client, within 200ms. Deploy and rollback repeatable. Injected failure alerts. Only internal traffic, feature flag, no real data. ✅\n\n✅ **Every task follows what blocks it**: Phase 0 (skeleton) → Phase 1 (validate CRDT / cost / scale assumptions) → Phase 2 (auth system) → Phase 3 (hardened backend) → Phase 4 (UI) → Phase 5 (sharing) → Phase 6 (history) → Phase 7 (scale) → Phase 8 (launch). No task proceeds without its dependencies passing gates. ✅\n\n✅ **Nothing reaches real users, data, or money before controls pass**: Real data (Phase 3) waits for auth (V5). Multi-user sharing (Phase 5) waits for permissions (V8). Public launch (Phase 8) waits for security audit (V11). ✅\n\n✅ **Rework runs widest first**: CRDT choice (V1) and self-hosted decision (V2) are evaluated first (Phase 1), before building implementation (Phase 3–4). Would touch entire backend if wrong. ✅\n\n✅ **Exposure runs smallest first**: Phase 0 internal-only → Phase 1–7 staging canary → Phase 8 public (canary 10% → 50% → 100%). ✅\n\n✅ **Each tradeoff has a row, tier, default, flip condition**: CRDT vs OT (R3, default CRDT, flip if V1 fails), self-hosted vs PaaS (R3, default self-hosted, flip if V2 fails), etc. ✅\n\n✅ **Each gate has five fields, phase, consumer**: V0–V11 each cite hypothesis, method, threshold, evidence, and unlocks. All critical: V0 (skeleton), V1–V4 (R3 decisions), V5 (auth before real data), V6–V10 (scale/performance), V11 (security before launch). ✅\n\n✅ **Each phase has all four cross-cutting moves**: Security, observability, reproducibility, resilience threaded through every phase from Phase 0, never deferred. ✅\n\n✅ **No AI layer**: N/A noted, not exempted silently. ✅\n\n✅ **Exceptions declared or noted**: None needed; skeleton and gating are not exceptions. ✅\n\n✅ **Deferred items named with pull-forward conditions**: Comments, templates, search, notifications, AI, API, PWA, mobile, federation—all named with condition (Phase X exit check passes, or business case). ✅\n\n---\n\n**END OF BUILD PLAN**","ttft_ms":77530,"type":"result","duration_ms":212875,"uuid":"4da0a001-9cbd-43d8-b688-a5768b5310f3","ttft_stream_ms":1343,"time_to_request_ms":86,"first_content_frame_ms":1344,"queued_turn_count":0,"result_index":0}
